# Dictionary (English–Garhwali–Hindi)

**By Dr. Achlanand Jakhmola**

*(Contains about 8,000 English and around 40,000 Garhwali and 32,000 Hindi equivalents)*

Dedicated to Garhwali Ancestry of Their Forefathers — of The culture and language — Lest they forget — of The present and future generations

Publisher & Printer: Samay Sakshya, 15, Falu Line, Dehradun, 0135 2658894
Email: mailssdun@gmail.com | Web: samaysakshya.com

## Why This Dictionary? (Preface)

This work has a distinctive purpose. I had started collecting Garhwali words with their English equivalents and vice-versa, just after the publication of my book 'Kosh Vidha' (Art of Dictionary Making) in 2004. This process, however, got a fillip after the bringing out of the voluminous 'Garhwali-Hindi-English Dictionary', prepared under my chief editorship and published by the Government of Uttarakhand in 2014. After that I received numerous calls from people of Garhwali origin, who were educated in English medium institutions and now living in towns and cities of India and abroad, urging me to make dictionary that would provide Garhwali equivalents for English words. Ironically, the majority among these comprised the youth from the Garhwal region itself, sadly including my progeny. They expressed a keen desire to learn Garhwali and further saying that they were fully conversant with the English language but hesitant to speak in their mother tongue Garhwali.

Besides, it is noticed that after the emergence of Uttarakhand in 2000, there is a great surge in the educated Garhwali youth to learn the language of their forefathers which is withering day by day. Even the basic kinship and pronominal terms of Garhwali are forgotten. Further, it is deeply distressing that UNESCO's 'Atlas of World's Languages in Danger' designates Garhwali as a language in the 'unsafe' category. Analysts have assigned numerous reasons for this alarming state, one very important cause of which is the non-availability of suitable bi-lingual dictionaries.

My tryst with lexicography, which began in 1958 after the University of Allahabad assigned me this subject for doctoral degree, was instrumental in introducing me to this genre. Thereafter, my interest in the technique of making lexicons, as discussed in the aforesaid book 'Kosh Vidha', in addition to the practical experience of compiling a dictionary, enthused me to undertake the present venture. I thought it worthwhile to contribute my mite, by creating a bi-lingual dictionary in which, along with common English words, would be provided their Garhwali equivalents and thus fill the void of non-availability of suitable dictionaries. I was also aware of the fact that every educated person of Garhwali ancestry is conversant with Hindi as well. As it is, a large number of words of Hindi have been assimilated in Garhwali, albeit with slight phonetic variations. Therefore, inclusion of meanings in Hindi would make the dictionary more advantageous in two ways: Firstly, it would give the Garhwali readers a wider scope to comprehend Garhwali words easily and secondly, the dictionary would also be useful to English-Hindi readers.

I started making plans for this dictionary and my first and foremost priority was the identification, selection and collection of such English words, which could be relevant and useful to the present generation and thereafter to find their equivalents having commonality of meaning in Garhwali and Hindi. I was aware of what C. K. Ogden and I.A. Richards had said long back in 'The Meaning of Meaning': No two words convey the same meaning. However, this formula does not apply strictly in the context of bi-lingual dictionaries, particularly in the case of visible objects, features, functions and activities. In a wider sense, some words or phrases having similarity in sense, idea, perception, trend, purport, tenor, gist, connotation, elucidation, or interpretation of purpose, are also included in the dictionary to clarify 'meaning'.

During the process of collecting words, I always kept in mind the dictum of the great Sanskrit lexicographer Mallinaath Suurii who had ordained that baseless, unwanted, obsolete and irrelevant words be avoided (नामूलं लिख्यते किंचिन्नापेक्षित उच्यते). Accordingly, I focussed on those common words of English language, whose equivalents in Garhwali would be useful for day to day needs or activities of the populace, besides meeting the social, cultural and, if possible, the professional requirements of the present generation. For this, although I had already collected quite a number of words earlier, I decided to augment the vocabulary. I scanned the abridged or student editions of renowned English dictionaries like Oxford, Cambridge, Chambers, Collins, and Macmillan etc. In addition, I also pored over the bi-lingual English-Hindi dictionaries, particularly of Dr. Raghuvira, Dr. Hardev Bahari, the Bhargava, Oxford English-Hindi and some Garhwali-Hindi, Kumaoni-Hindi and Rajasthani-Hindi dictionaries. Latest in this series was the aforementioned 'Garhwali-Hindi-English' dictionary. The present dictionary is, however, compiled with an altogether different objective, diverse plan, arrangement, approach, treatment, methodology and vocabulary.

Once the collection was over, I arranged the English words in alphabetical order and started putting the Garhwali and Hindi words against each. In my assemblage I found considerable number of Garhwali words of English origin, some of which albeit modulated by local phonetic tendencies. These words normally pertained to basic education sector e.g. school, master, copy, pencil, blackboard, class, pass, fail; popular sports such as ball, cricket, hockey; elementary entertainment: radio, transistor, television, cinema; local travel: ticket, bus, road, cycle, motor-cycle, car, truck; objects of daily use: phone, coat, pant, shirt, tie, cup, plate, gas, cylinder; elections and general administration: vote, member, judge, governor, collector, transfer, posting, file; commonly used terminology of defence forces and the like. I have included them in the dictionary in way they are spoken in Garhwali.

However, found that a huge number of English words in the pile, had no equivalents in Garhwali. Such words normally pertained to modern and advanced subjects such as information technology, engineering, multiple branches of science and medicine. Again it was difficult to find Garhwali correlatives of a number of English words connected with various segments of sports, nuances of politics, higher education, history, entertainment, literary and conversational terms, logic, philosophy, law, different fields of human knowledge and activity, serious thoughts, fashions, numerous facets of modernity etc. Therefore, all English words concerning these subjects had to be left out.

Garhwali is essentially a language of hill folks living in rural areas, and as such, closely associated with farming, animal husbandry and pastoral activities. The region is blessed with highly rich flora and fauna for which corresponding English words were non-existing. It was quite challenging to find correlating English words for numberless gods, goddesses, godlings, goblins, deities, objects of worship, concepts of numerous divinities and umpteen forms of their propitiations; faiths and beliefs, rituals, rites, traditions and ceremonies. Besides, there were countless terms connected with routine chores of peoples day to day and community life, family patterns, dimensions of human activities, means of livelihood; diseases and methods of their treatment; social customs, practices, rules of etiquette, attitudes, behaviour and actions of the populace largely inhabiting the villages of Garhwal for which English equivalents could not be found and therefore had to be abandoned.

The layout of this dictionary is very simple. Firstly, the main entry or catch word of English is indicated in bold; then is denoted its position as a part of speech or grammatical form, and thereafter are provided as many Garhwali words in italics and Hindi equivalents. In certain cases I could not find a proper Garhwali word conveying the same meaning as in English. For instance 'polygamy' and 'polyandry' a matrimonial custom, so prevalent in many border areas of Garhwal till a few years back, had no equivalent in Garhwali. In such cases I had to coin new phrases in the least possible letters. I have not ventured in giving explanation or elaboration of words either, as I did not want to land myself in controversy as happened with Dr. Samuel Johnson, the progenitor of English Dictionary, when he explained the word 'oats' in his Dictionary of the English Language as "a grain used for horses in England but for people in Scotland" which created a furore in the British parliament. Exigencies of space and paucity of resources did not warrant inclusion of other features provided in some larger or comprehensive dictionaries.

In this dictionary the Garhwali words are recorded in their spoken form as this language is not yet standardised. I have tried my best to maintain the peculiarities and tendencies of the Garhwali language in its original form. It is important to bear in mind that due to varying physiographical conditions, Garhwali has many dialects, sub-dialects and sub-sub-dialects reaching about twenty five. In many cases there are different dialectal variations for stress, tone, modulation, pronunciation and terminology for the identical object, idea or activity.

Furthermore, owing to diversities in climatic conditions, the vowels are pronounced differently even at small distances. Short and long vowels i.e. 'a-aa' (अ-आ), 'i-ii' (इ-ई), 'u-uu' (उ-ऊ) and some consonants like 'w'(व)-'b'(ब) and 'ka'(क)-'ga'(ग) as in बसगाळ–बसकाळ (rainy season), are interchangeable. Notably, there is a neutral vowel between 'a' (अ) and 'aa' (आ), phonetically transcribed as 'S' in Garhwali, the use of which makes a lot of semantic difference. There is predominance of ड़ for ड, retroflex 'N' (ण) in place of dental 'n' (न) and 'L' (ळ) for 'l' (ल). The sound 'rh' (ऱ्ह) is generally pronounced as (रि), 'ङ' as ('गं') and 'ञ' as ('यँ'). Among the fricatives, the alphabet's 'श' (श) is preferred to 'sh' (श) and 'Sh' (ष). The letter 'व' is pronounced as ('ख')in some areas.

Other tendency of Garhwali is predominance of short vowels. Way back around 200 BCE, sage Bharat Muni in his Natya Shastra had noted that the language of Himwant (Garhwal region) is 'उकारबहुला' i.e. tending towards 'u' or 'uu' (उ, ऊ). In this region, the final vowel in a large number of words, particularly nouns and verbs, tends towards 'u' (उ), 'uu' (ऊ) or 'o' (ओ), which are inter-changeable. For standardisation I have spelled the last vowel of all infinitive verbs in this dictionary as ending in 'uu' (ऊ) e.g. करणू (to do), जाणू (to go), खाणू(to eat) etc. Again in the use of voiceless unaspirated consonants, in place of voiceless aspirated consonants, is also prevalent, e.g. duud (दूद) in place of duudh (दूध) i.e. milk, and haat (हात) in place of haath (हाथ) i.e. hand. In syntax and a major part of grammar, Garhwali is generally similar to Hindi.

Not to exaggerate, it sometimes appears that the Garhwal has something special in its fertile soil. The rich vocabulary of Garhwali reveals that its sons like the great Kalidas, are very sensitive, emotional and nature-loving. They also exhibit a Shakespearian insight for penetrating into the human mind to depict all possible situations, predicaments, mannerisms and behavioural traits or gestures in different situations. For example the single English word 'coquetry' has seven variants in Garhwali: इतराट, फफराट, छकछयाट, छकनाट, नखरयाट, फैल, चोंचला; I wanted to acquaint the English educated present generation with some distinctive vocables of Garhwali, such as कळकळी, घुतघुती, भाउळी, छपछपी, खुद, सुगा and a number of other unique words, but could not find an apt or matching single word as 'key entry' or 'main entry' in English.

In this dictionary, in the case of a majority of English main entries, I have endeavoured to enlist as many equivalents of Garhwali and Hindi as possible, signifying the same meaning, purport, sense, idea, action or activity. To give only three samples from this dictionary, for the English word 'hastily' 20 equivalents of Garhwali are listed: सड़ग, सट-पट, अबि, लग्द-बग्द, लपालप, लपालपि, सट्ट, सटाक, सरसराक, सरासर, झटपट, खुडुक, तड़ाक, दनादन, पटापट, टपटप, फर-फर, एकदम, फट्ट; the word 'pierce' has 18 variations: खोसणू, ठोसणू, घुसाणू, घुबाणू, घुभाणू, बिंदणू, बिंधणू, घसुणू, कोचणू, पुड़ाणू, पुड़ेणू, घुभौंणू, छिराणू and the term 'blow' has 21: ठोकर, तमाचु, थप्पड़, खोप, गदक, चोट, घमाक, ठेस, दणाक, धप, पटाक, फचाक, फचाट, फटाक, लप्पड़, लाप्पड़, झटका, धक्का, छक्कड़, चमताळ, धक्कधक | As seen here, there are numerous Garhwali variants for a single English word. The list is very long. Similarly, a considerable number of Hindi equivalents of most of the English entries have also been given.

In this way, it is roughly estimated that the ratio of English-Garhwali-Hindi words should be 1 : 5 : 4 or so. Thus, for more than 8,000 of English, about 40,000 words of Garhwali and nearly 32,000 equivalents of Hindi have been included in this dictionary.

Making a dictionary is like constructing a building. Despite considerable pre-planning and the taking of all possible precautions during its coming up, a number of shortcomings and drawbacks are bound to be noticed after its completion. No dictionary can claim to be perfect and complete. This one is the work of a single person without any outside support. I have tried to make it as useful as possible within my limited resources. Nevertheless, one may still find errors and defects. I can only request the indulgent users to point out the shortcomings and suggest improvements:

गच्छतः स्खलनं क्वापि भवत्येव प्रमादतः ।
हसन्ति दुर्जनास्तत्र समादधति सज्जनाः ।

Before concluding I have to express my deep appreciation of my wife Smt. Shail, for smilingly ignoring my octogenarian eccentricities and wholeheartedly giving me her constant cooperation, encouragement and loving care, besides looking after me during my multiple illnesses. The credit of initiating this work goes to my son Roopak, who paved the way for its preparation. My daughter Rachna, irrespective of her busy schedule, came to my rescue whenever I needed her help, and diligently supervised the editing and revisions. My granddaughters Sharanya and Tara, helped me in learning how to use the computer and getting me past numerous bottlenecks, whenever I was stuck.

I extend my grateful thanks to Smt. Raanu Bisht, the skilful proprietor of the prestigious Samaya Sakshya, for printing and publishing this work and Shri Praveen Kumar Bhatt, the knowledgeable C.E.O. of this publishing house for competently completing the entire organising, setting, composing and printing job in a short period. Lastly, I cannot forget Shri Suresh Shah, who, while sitting by my side, assiduously typed and retyped this dictionary in five proofs for long months without demur.

— Achlanand Jakhmola

## Abbreviations Used in the Dictionary (संक्षिप्तियाँ)

| Abbr. | = | English | Hindi |
|---|---|---|---|
| adj. | = | Adjective | विशेषण |
| adv. | = | Adverb | क्रिया विशेषण |
| aux. v. | = | auxiliary verb | सहायक क्रिया |
| conj. | = | conjunction | संयोजक |
| e.g. | = | for example | उदाहरणार्थ |
| excl. | = | exclamation | विस्मयादिबोधक |
| i.e. | = | that is | अर्थात् |
| n. | = | noun (masculine, feminine) | संज्ञा |
| pref. | = | pref. | उपसर्ग |
| prep. | = | preposition | पूर्वसर्ग |
| pron. | = | pronoun | सर्वनाम |
| suf. | = | suffix | प्रत्यय |
| v. | = | verb (transitive/intransitive) | क्रिया |

## Dictionary Entries

### A

**A, a** — the first letter of the English alphabet.

**a** adj. *एक, क्वी;* एक, कोई, कुछ, एक ही प्रति।

**aback** adv. *पैथर, पैथरि, पिछ्वाड़ि, बौड़िक, खौळेक;* पीछे की ओर।

**abandon** v. *छुटौणू, ढुकराणू;* त्यागना, छोड़ना, तिरस्कार करना।

**abandoned** adj. *धुर्या, पातर, छ्वणी, छोड़्यूँ;* परित्यक्ता, परित्यक्त।

**abase** v. *बेइज्जति करणू, नाकदरि करणू;* अपमान करना, निरादर करना।

**abasement** n. *बेइज्जति;* अपमान, निरादर, अनादर।

**abash** v. *सरमा डालणू, चकमा देणू, घबरै दीणू, अळजाट में फंसौणू;* लज्जित करना, शरमाना।

**abask** n. *चटकताळि कु घाम;* तेज धूप।

**abate** v. *कम करणू, घटाणू;* न्यून होना, कम करना, घटना।

**abatement** n. *कमि;* न्यूनता, अल्पता।

**abattoir** n. *कसैघर, कसैखाना;* वधशाला, बूचड़खाना, कसाईखाना।

**abbess** n. *माई, नन;* महन्तिन, माँ, माता।

**abbey** n. *मारयूँ कु कूड़ू;* ईसाई मठ।

**abbot** n. *मठाधीस;* मठ का स्वामी, महन्त।

**abbreviate** v. *शब्दूँ तैं छ्वट करणू;* शब्दों को संक्षिप्त करना, लघु करना।

**abdicate** v. *राजगद्दि छोड़णू;* त्यागना, पद छोड़ना।

**abdication** n. *राजगद्दि छोड़ण कु काम;* सिंघासन का त्याग, पद त्याग।

**abdomen** n. *लदोड़, पटुकु, पेट, घेस, पोटयू;* उदर, पेट, तोंद।

**abdominal** adj. *लइडौ, लइड़कु;* उदर सम्बन्धी, पेट का।

**abduct** v. *हरेणू, हरेणो, भगैक लि जाणू, लूटिक लि जाणू, पुळैक भगाणू;* अपहरण करना, भगा ले जाना।

**abed** adj. *खटला मा;* बिस्तर पर, सेज पर।

**aberrant** adj. *कुकरमि;* धर्म भ्रष्ट।

**aberration** n. *कुकरम;* सनमार्ग से हटना, सत्पथ से विचलन।

**abetment** n. *हुस्क्याणौ काम;* बहकाव।

**abhor** v. *घिक्कारन्नू, घिणाणू;* कोसना, भर्त्सना करना।

**abhorrence** n. *धीण, झिड़झिड़ी;* घृणा, घिन।

**abhorrent** adj. *घिणौंण्या;* घिनौना।

**abide** v. *बुल्यूँ मानणू, बात मानणू;* बात मानना, टिकना।

**ability** n. *सिक्कल, सामर्थ, लयाँ, सकति, सक्या, समरत;* शक्ति, सामर्थ्य, क्षमता, योग्यता, बल।

**abject** adj. *निखतु, निकम्मु;* अधम, पतित, नीच।

**abjection** n. *हूँचपन;* नीचता।

**able** adj. *लैक, लैख, लायक, लायक;* काबिल, योग्य, उचित।

**ablush** adv. *सरमान्द दैं, झेंपते हुए, शरमाते हुए।*

**abnegate** v. *लप छ्वडणू;* त्यागना।

**abnormal** adj. *बेनियम, अनघड़;* असाधारण, रीति विरुद्ध।

**aboard** adv./prep. *जाज या गाडिमा चड़यूँ;* जहाज या गाड़ी में सवार।

**abode** n. *ड्यार, घर, घैर, घौर, घौर, ठिकाणु, भगवानै जगा, डेरा;* निवास स्थान, घर, मकान, देवस्थान।

**aboil** adj. *उबळदौ;* क्रोध से उबलता हुआ।

**abolish** v. *रीति-रिवाजौं तैं खतम करणू;* प्रथाओं को हटाना।

**abolition** n. *खतम, अन्त;* उन्मूलन, अन्त, समाप्ति।

**abominable** adj. *घिण्यूँ;* घृणित, धिनौना।

**abominate** v. *घिणौणू, हिरस करणू;* घृणा करना, द्वेष करना।

**abominatin** n./adj. *घीण, हिरस;* घृणा, द्वेष।

**aboriginal** n./adj. *जंगळि लोग;* आदिवासी।

**abort** v. *बच्चा गिराणू;* गर्भपात होना, पेट गिरना।

**abortion** n. *तूण, गरभपात;* गर्भपात।

**abortive** adj. *तुर्यूँ, अधुरू;* निष्फल, अधूरा।

**abound** v. *भौत होणू, बिन्दि लगणू;* उमड़ उठना, भरपूर होना, प्रचलित होना।

**about** adv./prep. *बारमाँ, नजीक, यनाँ-वनाँ, इनाँ-उनाँ, चौतरफाँ;* लगभग, के बारे में, विषय में, निकट, करीब-करीब, प्राय:।

**above** adv./prep. *माथि, माथिन, मथि, उब्ज, ऐंच;* ऊपर, शिखर पर, ऊँची ओर।

**above-all** adv./prep. *खास तौर पार;* विशेषकर, विशेषत:, सबसे बढ़कर।

**above-board** adv. *भलु, साफ;* बिना छल कपट के।

**abrade** v. *रगड़णू निचोड़णू करचोरणू;* खुरचना, रगड़कर हटाना, नष्ट करना।

**abrasion** n. *रगड़, खरोंच, रगड़, घर्षण।*

**abrasive** adj. *रगड़ण वाळु;* घिसने या रगड़ने वाला।

**abreast** adv. *यींतरफ-यींतरफ, दगड़ि;* बराबर, एक ही पंक्ति में, पीछे न रहते हुए।

**abridge** v. *छ्वटु करणू;* संक्षेप करना, कम करना।

**abridgement** n. *छ्वटु कर्यूँ;* संक्षेप, सार, संग्रह।

**abroad** adv. *भैर, देसन्दु, परदेस मा;* घर के बाहर, प्रदेश में, दूर देश में।

**abrogate** v. *कैं रिवाज तैं खतम करणू;* किसी नियम को समाप्त करना।

**abrupt** adj. *चडम, इकदम से;* अचानक, आकस्मिक, एकाएक।

**abruptly** adv. *अचानचकि;* अकस्मात, अचानक, एकाएक।

**abscess** n. *पवडा, पवाडा;* फोड़ा।
